<!-- page 1 -->
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, September 12, 2017
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, September 12, 2017, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board may meet in executive session pursuant to
A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s office
at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo
County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to 5
minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature and
will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be removed
from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: August 22, 2017 Regular Session; August 22, 2017 Executive Session; August 31,
2017 Regular Session; August 31, 2017 Executive Session; September 5, 2017 Regular Session;
and September 5, 2017 Executive Session
 
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 August 2017  
 
c. Constable Reports: Show Low Precinct #5 August and Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6 August
2017
 
 
d. Tax Exemptions with waiver filed pursuant to ARS §42-11153 as a request for redemption of
waiver
 
 
e. Adopt Resolution Number 28-17, cancelling the elections for Sky Hi DWID, Porter Mountain
DWID and Pinedale DWID and appointing the candidate(s) or deeming the position vacant
 
 
f. Adopt Resolution Number 29-17, cancelling the election for the White Mountain Fire Dist. and
appointing the candidate(s) or deeming the position vacant 
 
 
g. Approval of 20 Back Tax Land Parcels sold over the counter in the amount of $7,908.04

<!-- page 2 -->
h. Special Event Liquor License to White Mountain Firefighters Foundation for the 2017 Tour of
the White Mountains Bike Event to be held at the Pinetop Lakes Activity Center and Stables on
October 6 and 7, 2017
 
 
i. Conduct Special Election for the City of Winslow and Town of Pinetop-Lakeside on November
7, 2017
 
 
j. Expenditure of County Attorney RICO Funds for Show Low High School Baseball Booster
Club 
 
 
k. Contracts signed by Interim County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors Resolution:
Contract for Professional Services with Cannon Consultants, LLC for Woodruff Bridge over
Little Colorado River, Structure No. 9572 Post Design Services and a Professional Services
Agreement with Painted Sky Engineering & Survey, LLC for Little Mormon Lake Road
Striping & Signing Plan
 
 
3. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
 
a. Presentation to Marlinda Littleman for her partnership in the completion of Road 9062 in White
Cone and other projects within District II.
 
Presented By: Chairman Jesse Thompson
 
4. ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
 
a. Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions.
Presented By: Eric Scott
 
 
b. Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 30-17 to accept the grant award from Gila
Indian River Community for the Heber-Overgaard Fire District in the amount of $60,000 to
assist in purchasing a new ambulance.
 
Presented By: Chief Rich Upham
 
5. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board wishes
to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
1. Renewal of Agreement between The Arizona Early Childhood Development and Health Board
Navajo Nation Regional Partnership Council (First Things First) and Navajo County Public
Health Services District to provide nutrition education in the Navajo Nation region
 
 
2. Professional Services Agreement with Ellen S. Grabareck, RDH, for Dental Hygiene services
through First Things First,  Navajo Nation Oral Health Program effective July 1, 2017 to June
30, 2018 in the amount of $40.00/per hour
 
 
 
3. Professional Services Agreement with April Pohlman, RDH, for Dental Hygiene services

<!-- page 3 -->
3. Professional Services Agreement with April Pohlman, RDH, for Dental Hygiene services
through First Things First, Navajo Nation Oral Health Fluoride Program effective July 1,
2017 to June 30, 2018 in the amount of $40.00/per hour
 
 
4. Professional Services Agreement with Susan Dutton, RDH, for Dental Hygiene services
through First Things First, Navajo Nation Oral Health Fluoride Program effective July 1,
2017 to June 30, 2018 in the amount of $40.00/per hour
 
 
6. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. Consideration of award of Contract B17-07-020 Hot Asphaltic Concrete Pavement Overlay to
Sunland Asphalt & Construction, Inc.
 
Presented By: Jeanine Carruthers
 
b. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 31-17 
approving/denying a Special Use Permit to allow for the construction of a self-mini storage unit
facility that will accommodate a commercial buffer, individual storage units, and covered
storage spaces for boats and RVs on a parcel which is zoned C-R on the front half and
A-General for the back half, APN 409-20-015P, a portion of Township 10 North, Range 21
East, Section 4, in the Show Low area. 
 
Presented By: Bill Bess
 
c. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 32-17
approving/denying Final Plat Extensions on two (2) residential projects that were initiated in
2006, on a total of 46.14 acres currently zoned Special Development and R1-43, north of
Interstate 40 and west of Holbrook, on Township 18 North, Range 20 East, Section 33. 
 
Presented By: Bill Bess
 
7. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from Board members, County Attorney and Interim
County Manager.
 
 
 
8. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted:________ By:_____                         
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.d.        
Meeting Date: 09/12/2017  
Title: Possible approval of tax exemptions with waivers 
Submitted For: Marlene Sample, Chief Deputy Assessor 
Submitted By: Marlene Sample, Chief Deputy Assessor
Department: Assessor
Motion before the Board:
Tax Exemptions with waiver filed pursuant to ARS §42-11153 as a request for redemption
of waiver
Background:
Background:  Pursuant to ARS §42-11153, “If a widow, widower or disabled person whose
property is exempt from tax under section §42-1111, the person may have the waiver
redeemed by the County Board of Supervisors at any regular meeting, except that no taxes
that were due and payable before the petition was submitted may be refunded or abated.”
The following applications for tax exemption were filed past the March 1 deadline,
therefore, pursuant to ARS 42-11153, a “Request for Redemption of Waiver” has been
signed by each of the following:

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.e.        
Meeting Date: 09/12/2017  
Title: CA DWID Nov 2017
Submitted By: Rayleen Richards, Elections Director
Department: Elections
Motion before the Board:
Adopt Resolution Number 28-17, cancelling the elections for Sky Hi DWID, Porter
Mountain DWID and Pinedale DWID and appointing the candidate(s) or deeming the
position vacant
Background:
48-1012. Election of trustees; vacancies
 
E. If only one person files or no person files a nominating petition for election to fill a position on
the board of directors of the district, the county board of supervisors, by resolution, may cancel
the election for that office and appoint the person who filed the nominating petition to fill that
position. If no person files a nominating petition for an election to fill a district board office, the
county board of supervisors, by resolution, may cancel the election for those offices and those
offices are deemed vacant and shall be filled as otherwise provided by law. A person who is
appointed pursuant to this section is fully vested with the powers and duties of the office as if
elected to that office.
 
 
Attachments
CA DWID Doc 2017

<!-- page 390 -->
RESOLUTION NO ______ - 17 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS, CANCELLING THE ELECTIONS FOR 
SKY HI DWID, PORTER MOUNTAIN DWID AND PINEDALE DWID; AND APPOINTING THE CANDIDATE(S) OR 
DEEMING THE POSITION VACANT, PURSUANT TO ARS § 48-1012 (E) 
 
 
 
WHEREAS, ARS § 48-1012 (E) provides as follows with respect to Domestic Water Improvement District 
Board of Directors positions : 
 
“If only one person files or no person files a nominating petition for election to fill a position on the board of 
directors of the district, the county board of supervisors, by resolution, may cancel the election for that office and 
appoint the person who filed the nominating petitio n to fill that position. If no person files a nominating petition for an 
election to fill a district board office, the county board of supervisors, by resolution, may cancel the election for those 
offices and those offices are deemed vacant and shall be filled as otherwise provided by law. A person who is 
appointed pursuant to this section is fully vested with the powers and duties of the office as if elected to that office .” 
 
         and 
 
WHEREAS, A.R.S. § 16-410 (A) authorizes the Board to take such action no earlier than seventy -five (75) 
days before the election, and it is now less than seventy-five (75) days before Nov. 7, 2017 Special  Election at which 
the Domestic Water Improvement Districts Board of Directors will be elected;  
 
         and 
 
WHEREAS, the officer in charge of elections, has certified that the number of persons who filed nominating 
petitions to fill the Domestic Water Improvement Districts Board of Directors positions is less than or equal to the 
number of positions available, attached hereto and incorporated herein as “Exhibit A”;  
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED by the Board of Supervisors as follows:  
 
That the elections for the Pinedale DWID, Porter Mountain DWID and Sky HI DWID Board of Directors 
positions , attached hereto and incorporated herein as “Exhibit A”, are hereby cancelled by the Navajo County Board 
of Supervisors.

<!-- page 391 -->
PASSED, APPROVED AND ADOPTED at Holbrook, Arizona on September 12, 2017. 
 
  
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
 
                
 By ________________________________________ 
 Jesse Thompson  
 Chairman of the Board 
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
 
________________________________________ 
Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.f.        
Meeting Date: 09/12/2017  
Title: CA Fire Dist. Nov 2017
Submitted By: Rayleen Richards, Elections Director
Department: Elections
Motion before the Board:
Adopt Resolution Number 29-17, cancelling the election for the White Mountain Fire Dist.
and appointing the candidate(s) or deeming the position vacant 
Background:
 48-802. Election procedures
 
(D) 4. Except for an election to reorganize a fire district, nominating petitions shall be filed with
the board of supervisors as prescribed by title 16, chapter 3. If only one person files or no person
files a nominating petition for an election to fill a position on the district board or the position of
elected fire chief or elected secretary-treasurer for which the term of office is to expire, the board
of supervisors may cancel the election for that position and appoint the person who filed the
nominating petition to fill the position. If no person files a nominating petition for an election to
fill a district office, the board of supervisors may cancel the election for those offices and those
offices are deemed vacant and shall be filled as otherwise provided by law. A person who is
appointed pursuant to this paragraph is fully vested with the powers and duties of the office as if
elected to that office.

RESOLUTION NO ______ - 17 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS, CANCELLING THE ELECTIONS FOR 
THE WHITE MOUNTAIN FIRE DIST.; AND APPOINTING THE CANDIDATE(S) OR DEEMING THE POSITION 
VACANT, PURSUANT TO ARS § 48-802 (D)(4) 
 
 
 
WHEREAS, ARS § 48-802 (D)(4) provides as follows with respect to Fire District Board of Directors 
positions : 
 
“Except for an election to reorganize a fire district, nominating petitions shall be filed with the board of 
supervisors as prescribed by title 16, chapter 3. If only one person files or no person files a nominating petition for an 
election to fill a position on the district board or the position of elected fire chief or elected secretary -treasurer for 
which the term of office is to expire, the board of supervisors may cancel the election for that position and appoint the 
person who filed the nominating petition to fill the position. If no person file s a nominating petition for an election to fill 
a district office, the board of supervisors may cancel the election for those offices and those offices are deemed 
vacant and shall be filled as otherwise provided by law. A person who is appointed pursuant to this paragraph is fully 
vested with the powers and duties of the office as if elected to that office. ” 
 
         and 
 
WHEREAS, A.R.S. § 16-410 (A) authorizes the Board to take such action no earlier than seventy -five (75) 
days before the election, and it is now less than seventy-five (75) days before the November 7, 2017 General 
Election at which the Fire Districts Board of Directors will be elected;  
 
         and 
 
WHEREAS, the officer in charge of elections, has certified  that the number of persons who filed nominating 
petitions to fill the Fire Districts Board of Directors positions is less than or equal to the number of positions available,  
attached hereto and incorporated herein as “Exhibit A”  
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED by the Board of Supervisors as follows:  
 
That the elections for the White Mountain Fire Dist. Board Member positions , attached hereto and 
incorporated herein as “Exhibit A”, are hereby cancelled  and deemed elected or vacant by the Navajo County Board 
of Supervisors.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.h.        
Meeting Date: 09/12/2017  
Title: Special Event Liquor License - White Mountain Firefighters Foundation
Submitted By: Donna Hurstrom, Deputy Clerk of the Board
Department: Board of Supervisors
Motion before the Board:
Special Event Liquor License to White Mountain Firefighters Foundation for the 2017 Tour of the
White Mountains Bike Event to be held at the Pinetop Lakes Activity Center and Stables on
October 6 and 7, 2017
Background:
The White Mountain Firefighters Foundation is requesting a special event liquor license for the
2017 Tour of the White Mountains Bike Event to be held on October 6, 2017, from 2:00 p.m. to
9:00 p.m. and October 7, 2017, from 10:00 a.m. to 9:00 p.m., being held at the Pinetop Lakes
Activity Center & Stables, 5475 Bucksprings Rd, Pinetop, AZ  85935.  They will have 1 security
personnel available during the event and 2 personnel checking ID's and secure wristbands and 2
personnel who will distribute beer to individuals with event secured wristbands and signs posted
to reiterate that no alcoholic beverages shall leave special event at entry/exit points.

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE BOARD OF SUPERVISORS OF NAVAJO 
COUNTY, ARIZONA, ACCEPTING A GRANT FROM THE GILA RIVER 
INDIAN COMMUNITY FOR AN ABULANCE VEHICLE FOR THE 
HEBER-OVERGAARD FIRE DISTR ICT  
 
WHEREAS, the Heber-Overgaard Fire District seeks to obtain critically needed grant 
funding to replace an ambulance within their fleet; and  
 
WHEREAS, the Heber-Overgaard Fire District went through a detailed process of 
researching, drafting, compiling, and processing all the necessary materials to complete the grant 
being proposed to the  Gila River Indian Community, and the Navajo County Board of 
Supervisors, acting as the sponsoring entity of the grant, encourages and supports this type of 
proactive work; and 
 
WHEREAS, the grant application completed by the Heber-Overgaard Fire District 
fulfills the priority areas identified by the Gila River Indian Community for awarding such types 
of grants to governmental agencies, as this grant proposal will serve the Heber-Overgaard Fire 
District, a certified fire district in the State of Arizona; and;  
 
WHEREAS, it is the desire of the Board of Supervisors to support and accept the 
awarded grant for the Heber-Overgaard Fire District by the Gila River Indian Community; and 
 
WHEREAS, it is the desire of the Bo ard of Supervisors to offer their appreciation to the 
Gila River Indian Communities for their generosity in offering these grant opportunities to fire 
district agencies located within the State of Arizona; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Gila River Indian Community grant application process requires a 
resolution from the Navajo County Board of Supervisors for the grant application to be awarded. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that the Navajo County Board of 
Supervisors does hereby lend its full support and approval, and accepts the awarded amount of 
the grant, as the sponsoring entity for the Heber-Overgaard Fire District, by the Gila River Indian 
Community for the purchasing of an ambulance.  
 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED at Holbrook, Arizona, on September______, 2017.

Motion before the Board:
Renewal of Agreement between The Arizona Early Childhood Development and Health Board
Navajo Nation Regional Partnership Council (First Things First) and Navajo County Public
Health Services District to provide nutrition education in the Navajo Nation region
Background:
Recommend approving the Government Agreement.  This is a contract through First Things First,
and would be a renewal of the contract we've had with them for the past three years. The intent of
this evidence based nutrition, obesity and physical activity strategy is to provide nutrition
education to 2000 children birth through 5 years of age, and 4000 adults in the Navajo Nation
region, particularly as part of a comprehensive and coordinated system.  The expected results are
the reduction in the prevalence of obesity rates within children and their family members, in order
to prevent lifelong complications to health and wellbeing.
The term of this agreement is July 1, 2017 through June 30, 2018.

                   Activity Amount Available for Award:  $582,930

Brief Description: 
The intent of this evidence based Nutrition, Obesity and Physical Activity strategy is to provide nutrition 
education to 2000 children birth through 5 years of age, and 4000 adults in the Navajo Nation region, 
particularly as part of a comprehensive and coordinated system. The expected results are the reduction 
in the prevalence of obesity rates within children and their family members, in order to prevent lifelong 
complications to health and wellbeing.

requested/granted.  Further instruction will be provided closer to award.

GOVERNMENT AGREEMENT 
 
GRA-RC001-18-0884-01 
 
Between The 
Arizona Early Childhood Development and Health Board  
Navajo Nation Regional Partnership Council 
(First Things First)  
And  
Navajo County Public Health Services District  
(Grantee) 
 
I. Purpose 
 
The purpose of this grant is to specify the responsibilities and procedures for the grantee 
role in administration of funds provided by First Things First. 
 
II. Term, Renewal 
 
The term of this Agreement is July 1, 2017 through June 30, 2018 with no option to renew.   
 
III. Description of Services  
 
A. Statement of Need 
 
According to the Pediatric Nutrition Surveilance System (PedNSS), the rate of 
overweight children ages 2 to 5 in 2011 was 15.5% (compared to the national average 
of 16%) and the obesity rate was at 14.5% (compared to 14.4% nationally). While local 
rates of obesity and being overweight are at or under the national average, Arizona is 
not showing any gains made over the years and in fact has shown increases (from an 
overweight rate of 12.8% in 2002 to 15.5% in 2011, and from an 11.4% obesity rate in 
2002 to 14.5% in 2011). In addition, overweight and obesity rates amongst Native 
Americans in Arizona is significantl y higher, especially amongst children. 
   
B. Strategy Description  
 
The aim of this strategy is to provide evidence-based community and place-based 
health education focused on obesity prevention to children, families and early child 
care and education professionals to increase opportunities for children to have a 
healthy life and that parents and caregivers, schools, communities support nutrition 
and physical activity behaviors that support children in achieving a healthy weight.

<!-- page 439 -->
Page 3 of 27 
 
C. Target Population  
 
The target population and geographic area to be served includes children birth to age 5, 
their parents/caregivers, expectant mothers and early care and education professionals 
within the regional boundaries of the Navajo Nation Regional Partnership Council as 
detailed below in the Scope of Work section of this Agreement. 
 
IV. GRANTEE’S Responsibilities 
 
The Grantee shall: 
 
A. Prior to entering into this Agreement, have completed and submitted to First Things 
First for review and approval the following forms and documents: 
1. Program Personnel Table/Program Organization Chart 
2. Line-Item Budget and Budget Narrative 
The completed forms and documents comprise part of this Agreement. 
B. In providing programming described in Section IV.A, the Grantee shall act in accordance 
with its Program Questions and Narrative Responses ; the approved budget; and the 
following First Things First documents: the Scope of Work (Exhibit A), Guidance 
Materials (Exhibit B), and the Data Security Guidelines and Requirements for 
Collaborators (Exhibit C).   
C. Coordinate and collaborate with all First Things First grant recipients, as collaboration is 
critical to developing a seamless service delivery system for children and families. 
D. Submit timely the reports described in Section VI. 
 
V. Reimbursement/Payment  
 
A. First Things First shall pay the Grantee on a cost-reimbursement basis for expenses 
approved in the budget, up to $582,930 on the terms described in this Section. 
 
B. Payment is conditioned upon receipt by First Things First of timely, accurate and 
complete (i) reimbursement documents, (ii) Program Narrative Reports and (iii) Data 
Submission Reports submitted via the First Things First Partner Grant Management 
System (PGMS).  Payments shall be made only for those services performed or goods 
received.  
 
C. The Grantee shall submit reimbursement requests at least quarterly, though not more 
frequently than monthly.  The Grantee shall submit a final reimbursement request 
marked “final” no more than forty -five (45) days after the Agreement end date.  
Expenses eligible for reimbursement must be paid, accrued or obligated by the Grantee 
by the Agreement term end date.  Final payment shall be contingent upon receipt of all 
fiscal, programmatic, and data reports required of the Grantee under this Agreement.

<!-- page 440 -->
Page 4 of 27 
 
Requests for reimbursement received later than forty-five (45) days after the 
Agreement end date will not be paid.  
 
D. Funds provided to the Grantee under this Agreement shall only be used to fulfill the 
Grantee’s responsibilities under this Agreement.  Any questions regarding the 
appropriate use of the funds shall be resolved by mutual agreement between the 
parties. 
 
E. If the Grantee receives reimbursement for expenditures that are disallowed by an audit 
exception by First Things First, the state or the federal government, the Grantee shall 
promptly repay the funds to First Things First. 
 
VI. Quarterly Program Narrative and Data Submission Reporting Requirements  
 
A. At a minimum, the Grantee shall submit quarterly one Program Narrative Report and 
one Data Submission Report by the 20th of the month following the quarter via PGMS.  
Failure to submit timely reports will result in suspension of reimbursement.  The 
reports shall contain such information as deemed necessary by First Things First. 
 
B. Program Narrative and Data Submission Reports are due: 
 
1st Quarter (July 1 - September 30)  Due:  October 20 
2nd Quarter (October 1 - December 31)  Due:  January 20 
3rd Quarter (January 1 - March 31)  Due:  April 20 
4th Quarter (April 1 - June 30)   Due:  July 20 
 
C. If the Grantee provides services to more than one First Things First region (multi-
regional strategies), the Grantee shall collect, store and report the data for the Data 
Submission Reports separately for each region served. 
 
VII. General Terms 
 
A. FTF Grants Uniform Terms and Conditions.  First Things First’s Grants Uniform Terms 
and Conditions (revision date November 2015) are hereby incorporated by reference 
into this Agreement as if fully set forth herein.  Copies of this document are available by 
emailing the First Things First Procurement Specialist, grants@azftf.gov. 
 
B. Working on Tribal Land.  If the Grantee performs any work under this Agreement on 
sovereign land of an Indian tribe or nation, the Grantee shall comply with any 
requirements set forth by the tribal government in relation to essen tial functions of the 
grant operation, including data collection.  It is a material requirement of this 
Agreement that the Grantee follow all First Things First tribal policies and procedures 
including the Tribal Data Policy, complete all Institutional Review Board (IRB)

<!-- page 441 -->
Page 5 of 27 
 
requirements, obtain all appropriate parental consents and obtain appropriate tribal 
approvals as designated by tribal authorities. 
 
C. Non-Discrimination.  The provisions of State Executive Order 2009-09 are incorporated 
herein by reference.  These provisions mandate, in part, that contractors will not 
discriminate against any employee or applicant for employment because of race, age, 
color, religion, sex or national origin.  The Grantee shall also comply with all other 
applicable state and federal statutes, regulations and executive orders concerning non-
discrimination practices, including the Americans with Disabilities Act and Federal 
Executive Order No. 13279 – Equal Protection of the Laws for Faith-Based and 
Community Organizations. 
 
D. Records.  Pursuant to A.R.S. § 8-1174, the Grantee shall retain and shall contractually 
require each subcontractor and subgrantee to retain all books, accounts, reports, files 
and other records (“records”) relating to the Agreement for a period of five years after 
the completion of the Agreement.  All records shall be subject to inspection and audit 
by the State (including First Things First) and by an independent auditor at all 
reasonable times.  Upon request, the Grantee shall produce any or all such records at 
First Things First’s main office in Phoenix, Arizona. 
 
Notwithstanding the foregoing paragraph, pursuant to 2 C.F.R. § 200.333, if the grant 
includes federal pass-through funds, then the Grantee shall retain and shall 
contractually require each subcontractor and subgrantee to retain all records 
pertaining to the federal pass -through funds for a period of three years from the date 
of submission of the final expenditure report and until any litigation, claims or audit 
findings involving the records have been resolved and final action taken.  All such 
records shall be accessible and subject to audit in accordance with 2 C.F.R. § 200.336.  
This paragraph does not apply to a grantee, subgrantee or subcontractor that is a 
federal agency. 
 
E. Non-Availability of Funds.  Every payment obligation of First Things First under this 
Agreement is conditioned upon the availability of funds appropriated or allocated for 
the payment of such obligation.  If funds are not allocated and available for the 
continuance of this Agreement, this Agreement may be terminated by First Things First 
at the end of the period for which funds are available.  No liability shall accrue to First 
Things First in the event this provision is exercised, and First Things First shall not be 
obligated or liable for any future payments or for any damages as a result of 
termination under this paragraph. 
 
F. Relationship of Parties. The Grantee under this Agreement is an independent contractor.  
Neither party to this Agreement shall be deemed to be the employee or agent of the 
other party. 
 
VIII. Agreement  Administration and Operation s

<!-- page 442 -->
Page 6 of 27 
 
 
A. Advertising, Publishing and Promotion of Agreement.  The Grantee shall not use, 
advertise or promote information for commercial benefit concerning this grant without 
the prior written approval of First Things First. 
 
B. Review of Printed Materials.  First Things First must review and approve all Grantee 
publications and/or media funded or partially funded through this Agreement for 
compliance with this Agreement.  The Grantee shall submit to First Things First via 
PGMS all print and electronic materials related to the programs and services funded 
under this Agreement before publicly distributing those materials so that First Things 
First may first review and approve prior to release.  If deemed necessary by First Things 
First, the Grantee shall revise the materials as indicated by First Things First before 
publicly distributing the materials.  First Things First shall have full and complete rights 
to reproduce, duplicate, disclose, perform, and otherwise use all materials prepared 
under this Agreement. 
 
C. Acknowledgment of FTF Funding.  The Grantee shall recognize First Things First as a 
funding source of programs and services funded in whole or part under this Agreement 
in all publicly distributed print or electronic materials related to those programs and 
services.  The Grantee shall make this recognition in a manner described in First Things 
First’s most current protocol and style guide.  First Things First will post any updates to 
the protocol and style guide under the Grantee Resources section of PGMS.   The 
Grantee shall also recognize First Things First as a funding source of programs and 
services funded in whole or part under this Agreement in all formal oral presentations 
and media interviews related to those programs and services .   
 
D. Public Awareness Efforts.  The Grantee shall consult with First Things First in the 
planning of public awareness/marketing strategies, such as websites, advertising or 
media campaigns, related to the programs or services funded under this Agreement. 
 
E. Property of the State.  Any materials and data required to be collected, delivered or 
created under this Agreement, including  but not limited to reports, computer programs 
and other deliverables, are the sole property of the State (First Things First) .  The 
Grantee is not entitled to a patent or copyright on those materials and may not transfer 
the patent or copyright to anyone else.  The Grantee shall not use or release these 
materials without the prior written consent of First Things First. 
 
F. Ownership of Intellectual Property.  Any and all intellectual property, including but not 
limited to copyright, invention, trademark, trade name, service mark, and/or trade 
secrets created or conceived pursuant to or as a result of this Agreement and any 
related subcontract or subgrant (“Intellectual Property”), shall be work made for hire 
and First Things First shall be considered the creator of such Intellectual Property.  First 
Things First shall own (for and on behalf of the State) the entire right, title and interest 
to the Intellectual Property throughout the world.  The Grantee shall notify First Things

<!-- page 443 -->
Page 7 of 27 
 
First, within thirty (30) days, of the creation of any Intellectual Property by it or its 
subcontractor(s) and subgrantee(s). The Grantee, on behalf of itself and any 
subcontractor(s) and subgrantee(s), agrees to execute any and all document(s) 
necessary to assure ownership of the Intellectual Property vests in the State and shall 
take no affirmative actions that might have the effect of vesting all or part of the 
Intellectual Property in any entity other than the State. The Intellectual Property shall 
not be disclosed by Grantee or its subcontractor(s) and subgrantee(s) to any entity not 
the State without the express written authorization of First Things First. 
 
G. Sectarian Purposes.  Funds provided under this Agreement may not be expended for 
any sectarian purpose or activity, including religious worship or instruction.  
Additionally, the Grantee shall implement the programs or services funded under this 
Agreement consistent with the First Amendment of the United States Constitution.  
With respect to these programs or services, the Grantee also shall not discriminate 
against any program or service beneficiary or applicant on the basis of religion.  First 
Things First reserves the right to verify or monitor compliance with this paragraph.  The 
Grantee shall repay any funds awarded under this Agreement that the Grantee spends 
in violation of this paragraph. 
 
IX. Indemnification  
 
A. Not State Agency.  This paragraph applies if the Grantee is not a department, agency,

against any and all claims, actions, liabilities, damages, losses, costs or expenses 
(including reasonable attorneys’ fees) (hereinafter collectively referred to as “Claims”)

B. Patent and Copyright.  The Grantee shall indemnify and hold harmless the State against

apply if the Grantee is insured pursuant to A.R.S. § 41-621. 
 
C. Subcontractors.  The Grantee shall contractually require its subcontractors and

Administrative/Indirect Costs:  Administrative costs are general or centralized expenses of overall 
administration of an agency/organization that receives grant funds and does not include particular 
program costs.  Such costs are generally identified with the agency/organization’s overall operation and 
are further described in 2 CFR 220, 2 CFR 225, and 2 CFR 230. 
 
Indirect costs are costs of an organization that are not readily assignable to a particular program, but 
are necessary to the operation of the organization and the performance of the program.  The cost of 
operating and maintaining facilities, depreciation, and administrative salaries are examples of the types 
of costs that are usually treated as indirect. 
 
Applicants must list either Option A or Option B and provide proper justification for expenses 
included: 
 
 Option A - Administrative Costs:  with proper justification, grantees may include an allocation 
for administrative costs for up to 10% of the total direct costs requested of the grant request.  
Administrative costs may include allocable direct charges for:  costs of financial, accounting, 
auditing, contracting or general legal services; costs of internal evaluation, including overall 
management improvement costs; and costs of general liability insurance that protects the 
agency/organization(s) responsible for operating a program, other than insurance costs solely 
attributable to the program.  Administrative costs may also include that portion of salaries and 
benefits of the program’s director and other administrative staff not attributable to the time 
spent in support of a specific program. 
OR 
● Option B - Federally Approved Indirect Costs:  If your agency/organization has a federally 
approved indirect cost rate agreement in place, grantees may include an allocation for indirect 
costs for up to 10% of the direct costs.  Applicants must provide a copy of their federally 
approved indirect cost rate agreement.

Attachment F  
 
Exhibit A – Overview of First Things First and Scope of Work  
First Things First is designed to meet the diverse needs of children and families in Arizona 
communities. The statewide First Things First Board and Regional Partnership Councils in local 
communities across the state share the responsibility of ensuring that early childhood funds are 
spent on strategies that will result in improved development, health and education outcomes 
for young children.  
 
Local Regional Partnership Councils are comprised of community volunteers, with each member 
representing a specific segment of the community that has a role in ensuring that Arizona’s 
children grow up to be ready for school and set for life:  parents, leaders of faith communities, 
tribal representatives, educators, health professionals, business leaders and philanthropists.   
 
First Things First Strategic Direction  
First Things First’s commitment to young children means more than only funding programs and 
services.  It means having a shared vision about what being prepared for kindergarten actually 
means.  First Things First specifies that programs and services funded by the First Things First 
Board and Regional Partnership Councils are to address one or more of the following Goal 
Areas to impact children birth to age five and their families: 
 Improve the quality of early childhood development and health programs 
 Increase the access to quality early childhood development and health programs 
 Increase access to preventive health care and health screenings  
 Offer parent and family support and education concerning early childhood development 
and literacy 
 Provide professional development and training for early childhood development and 
health providers 
 Increase coordination of early childhood development and health programs and provide 
public information about the importance of early childhood development and health 
 
The First Things First Board established a strategic framework with a set of school readiness 
indicators that provide a comprehensive composite measure to show if Arizona is making 
progress in providing opportunity for young children to be ready for school and set for life.  The 
strategies funded by First Things First work collectively to develop a comprehensive system 
across the state and regionally to address the school readiness indicators.  The First Things First 
Board and Regional Partnership Councils determine the priorities and strategies to be funded 
across the state and throughout the regions assessing the challenges and building on the 
resources and assets in place. 
 
School Readiness Indicators  
 #/% children demonstrating school readiness at kindergarten entry in the development 
domains of social-emotional, language and literacy, cognitive and motor and physical.

<!-- page 456 -->
GRA-RC001-18-0884-01        Page 20 of 27 
 #/% of children enrolled in an early care and education program with a Quality First 
rating of 3-5 stars. 
 #/% of children with special needs enrolled in an inclusive early care and education 
program with a Quality First rating of 3-5 stars. 
 #/% of families that spend no more than 10% of the regional median family income on 
quality care and education with a Quality First rating of 3-5 stars. 
 % of children with newly identified developmental delays during the kindergarten year. 
 #/% of children entering kindergarten exiting preschool special education to regular 
education. 
 #/% of children ages 2-4 at a healthy weight (Body Mass Index-BMI). 
 #/% of children receiving at least six well child visits within the first 15 months of life. 
 #/% of children age 5 with untreated tooth decay. 
 % of families who report they are competent and confident about their ability to 
support their child’s safety, health and wellbeing. 
 
Scope of Work  
 
Statement of Need  
In 2015, Trust for America’s Health “The State of Obesity” reported the number of obese adults 
has grown dramatically in Arizona over the past 15 years, and is expected to grow signifi cantly 
in the next 20 years.  The adult obesity rates in Arizona increased from 14.9 percent in 2000 to 
28.7 percent in 2015.  For Native Americans in Arizona, the obesity rate was significantly higher, 
reporting 81 percent of Native Americans and Alaska Natives in Arizona were obese or 
overweight in 2013. 
 
The Center for Disease Control’s National Health and Nutrition Examination Survey reported 
rates have become much higher starting in earlier ages — 8.9 percent of 2- to 5-year-olds are 
now obese, and approximately 2 percent are extremely obese. In addition, Indian Health 
Services reported among American Indian/Alaska Native children, 25 percent of 2- to 5-year-
olds, 31 percent of 6- to 11-year-olds and 31 percent of 12- to 19-year-olds are obese. 
A 2011 Arizona Department of Health Services Obesity Epidemic report states, “31 percent of 
the children age two through four participating in Arizona Women, Infants, and Children (WIC) 
program in 2009 were either obese (14 percent) or overweight (16 percent).  Among Native 
American children in WIC, 38 percent were either overweight or obese.”   
 
The high prevalence of obesity of all ages and genders in Native American communities is a 
concern for the Navajo Nation Regional Partnership Council.  The childhood obesity epidemic 
directly leads to many other serious health problems such as type 2 diabetes, metabolic 
syndrome, high blood pressure, asthma, and other respiratory problems, sleep disorders, liver 
disease, heart disease, eating disorders and skin infections.  Research indicates obesity has 
become a major health problem among Native Americans in the past one to two generations 
which is associated with the relative abundance of high-fat foods and the rapid change from 
active to sedentary lifestyles.

<!-- page 457 -->
GRA-RC001-18-0884-01        Page 21 of 27 
 
Preventing obesity early can impact a child’s lifetime trajectory. A study of more than 7,700 
children found that a third of the children who were overweight in kindergarten were obese by 
eighth grade. Overweight 5-year-olds were more than four times as likely as healthy weight 
children to become obese. Therefore, it is important to develop specific prevention approaches 
aimed towards families with children 0 through age five, including pregnant women.  
 
The Navajo Nation Regional Partnership Council’s intent is to support a comprehensive 
evidence-based approach to improve nutrition and increase physical activity in early learning 
programs and to influence families with children 0 through age five to establish healthy habits 
to reduce obesity -related diseases.  It is  essential to target young children, because 
developmentally, young children are at a critical stage for learning health information and skills, 
which will help to establish a healthy lifestyle.   
 
Description of Strategy  
The purpose of this strategy is to ultimately reduce the risk factors for poor nutrition, poor 
health and insufficient physical activity of children birth to age 5. The mechanism employed to 
reach these goals is to provide interactive nutrition education directly to the prenatal 
population, children in the community, their parents/caregivers and early care and education 
professionals. While an evidence based program model is preferred, some evidence informed 
and promising practice models will be accepted. Each target population must be served through 
a curriculum or model that is designed specifically for that audience. 
 
The education must be provided within community settings and be interactive in nature 
whereby individuals are actively engaged in their learning about adopting healthy nutrition and 
eating habits, maintaining an appropriate weight and boosting their daily/weekly physical 
activity. This strategy is aimed at encouraging behavior change so that children can learn how 
they can initiate their own changes in their eating and physical activity, and parents and 
caregivers can learn their capacity to influence a positive change their child’s behavior.  
 
The implementation of this strategy must include three proven areas directly linked to obesity 
prevention: nutrition, physical activity and limiting screen time.  
 
1. Empower Parents and Families 
 Provide education, information and support to parents to promote healthy eating 
and physical activity among young children and reduce the incidence of overweight 
and obesity 
 Provide education, information and support to parents to limit screen time, and 
increase physical activity for their children 
 Coordinate with the existing Baby Friendly initiative to increase the initiation and 
duration of breastfeeding 
 Coordinate nutrition education activities  with family support strategies such as 
home visitation, food security, etc.

<!-- page 458 -->
GRA-RC001-18-0884-01        Page 22 of 27 
2. Early Learning Programs Preventive Services 
 Provide education, information and support to early care and education 
professionals to promote healthy eating and physical activity among young children  
 Provide education to early care and education professionals about limiting screen 
time for children and increasing physical activity as part of the curriculum 
 Measure effectiveness of interventions as part of the quality improvement efforts 
 Coordinate nutrition education activities with other early learning strategies such as 
Quality First, Child Care Health Consultation, Family, Friends, and Neighbor, etc.  
 
3. Community-Based Preventive Services  
 Expand or create nutrition and physical activity programs in the communities 
targeted to children ages birth through five and pregnant women 
 Implement interventions targeting nutrition, healthy weight and physical exercise 
into existing community services for children birth through five. 
 Provide health education media messages regarding healthy weight and physical 
activity 
 
All services provided by the grantee should be provided in a manner that is consistent with 
and reflective of the culture and beliefs of the population of the Navajo Nation. 
 
Applicable School Readiness Indicators  
Partners implementing this strategy will work collectively with First Things First to address the 
school readiness indicators below:   
 #/% of children ages 2-4 at a healthy weight (Body Mass Index-BMI). 
 
Applicable Goa l Areas  
Partners implementing this strategy will work collectively with First Things First to address the 
goal areas below:  
 Improve the quality of early childhood development and health programs 
 Increase the access to quality early childhood development and health programs 
 Increase coordination of early childhood development and health programs and provide 
public information about the importance of early childhood development and health 
 
Target Population and Geographic Area to Serve  
The target population for this strategy includes children 0-5, parenting adults of children 0-5, 
pregnant women and early care and education professionals within the service area for the 
Navajo Nation Regional Partnership Council in Arizona. The grantee must target families wi th 
children 0-5 and pregnant women who are at risk for obesity, diabetes or poor physical activity 
and nutrition. 
 
The Navajo Nation Regional Partnership Council provides services throughout the tribal lands 
of the Navajo Nation within Arizona. Programs funded under this Agreement must provide 
services throughout the region.

<!-- page 459 -->
GRA-RC001-18-0884-01        Page 23 of 27 
 
Please note Regional Partnership Council boundaries are set by the Board of First Things First 
every two years.  The Board  of First Things First approved updated Regional Partnership 
Council boundaries for FY2015 and beyond.  Please use the following “mapping resource” to 
see a visual picture of the Regional Partnership Councils and/or search for a council by address: 
http://maps.azftf.gov 
 
Target Service Units and Performance Measures  
A Target Service Unit (TSU) is a First Things First designated indicator of performance specific to 
each First Things First strategy and it is set by the Regional Council.  It is composed of a unit of 
measure (e.g., participating adults) and a target number.  The unit of measure can be a target 
population (e.g., participating adults), a product (e.g., books distributed) or a service (e.g., 
fluoride varnishes applied to children, ages 0-5) that a grantee is expected to serve as part of an 
agreement.  The target number represents the actual number of service units proposed to be 
delivered during the contract year.  
 
Performance Measures are (1) key indicators of performance (Target Service Units); (2) basic 
implementation of strategy; (3) alignment of program activities to strategy specific standards of 
practice, (4) performance or progress toward pre-established strategic goals.  Performance 
measures may include the level or type of program activities conducted (e.g. serving 
families/children through home visits) and/or the direct services and products delivered by a 
program (e.g., providing scholarships).  
 
For more specific information about the Target Service Units for this strategy, refer to the 
Guidance Materials section of this Agreement to find the link to the Target Service Unit 
Guidance Document.  
 
The Target Service and Performance Measures for this strategy are: 
Target Service Units: 
     Number of participating children: 2000 
     Number of participating adults: 4000 
 
Performance Measures: 
     Number of participating adults 
     Number of participating children

Exhibit C 
 
Data Security Guidelines and Requirements for Collaborators 
 
BACKGROUND:  
The purpose of the Arizona Early Childhood Development and Health Board (First Things First - FTF) is to 
aid in the creation of a system that offers opportunities and support for families and communities in the 
development of all children, so they can grow up healthy and ready to succeed. Our work is accountable 
and transparent to decision-makers and the citizens of Arizona. Collaboration and direct funding of 
grantees to undertake work on behalf of the children and families of Arizona is fundamental to the 
purpose and mission of FTF. Regular submission of data related to funded work is an important part of 
ensuring accountability and maximum positive impact for young children, as well as a material condition 
of receiving FTF grant funding.  
 
Data Security Guidelines for Data Submission to FTF 
First Things First will ensure that resources allocated have maximum impact for the benefit of children 
and families. To ensure this accountability, FTF has established data reporting requirements for all state 
and regional grantees. All funded providers shall regularly submit programmatic and financial reports as 
identified in the FTF reporting requirements.  
 
FTF data submissions are classified in one of three levels:   
 Public data 
 Limited distribution data 
 Confidential data 
 
Public data are those data that are readily available in the public sphere, such as websites, publications, 
or other widely-used sources. Limited distribution data do not identify individuals, but may be of 
sufficiently small cell size that their dissemination poses a threat to the confidentiality of individuals. 
Confidential data are those data that identify individuals; are governed by tribal or other agreements 
that limit their viewing, analysis, and dissemination; or that even when aggregated, put at risk the 
anonymity of any individual. 
 
The majority of FTF reporting submissions are completed through the FTF Partner Grant Management 
System (PGMS). Subsequent to the award of a grant, the grantee will receive general training on login 
and navigation within the PGMS system. With this login, the grantee will be able to manage their 
contract information. An additional training on strategy-specific data submission requirements will also 
be conducted. During that training, the grantee will be informed on submission of data reporting 
requirements through PGMS. All data submitted through PGMS is public data or limited distribution 
data.  Because PGMS is located in a secure extranet environment, grantees using PGMS for data 
submission are not required to undertake additional security measures related to their data submission 
above those identified in the general and data submission orientations (password and login security, 
guidelines for upload of narrative and other reports).  
 
A small group of grantees submit data requirements, with an agreement between the grantee and FTF, 
through an established secure web service or FTP (File Transfer Protocol) site via the internet, rather

<!-- page 462 -->
GRA-RC001-18-0884-01        Page 26 of 27 
than a PGMS web-based entry form. Such data is likely to contain limited distribution data and shall 
adhere to the following protocols. Grantees that submit data through the secure web service must 
submit data within the established data structures and format; follow all login procedures; submit a 
formal data change request form if needed; and ensure that limited distribution data may not be 
intercepted or viewed at any time by parties other than the grantee and FTF.  Additionally, grantees 
must ensure that throughout the reporting and submission process the data is secured and that any 
confidential data is de-identified and/or encrypted. 
 
Any grantee submitting data identified as confidential must file a formal data security policy with FTF.  
 
Data Security Guidelines for Grantee Maintenance of Data  
In order to submit data to FTF in fulfillment of reporting requirements, grantees shall keep all data 
collected for their program(s) within their system (database) or hardcopies. Grantee data is likely to 
contain highly sensitive information on individuals, their education and their health. These guidelines 
and requirements are for the maintenance of those data.  
 
All grantees must have a data security policy in force that identifies how the organization ensures that 
data is protected in all its forms, during all phases of its life cycle, from inappropriate access, use, 
modification, disclosure, or destruction.  
 
All grantees subject to HIPAA, FERPA, tribal law, or other data regulation are required to submit and 
maintain those approvals for all data. 
 
Data Permission Guidelines for Grantee Data 
All grantees must be prepared for FTF review of client-level data (e.g. child-level, teacher-level, or early 
care and education provider-level) during on-site visits. Additionally, FTF data reporting requirements 
may include submission of client-level data (e.g. child-level, teacher-level, or early care and education 
provider-level). The grantee agrees to allow FTF to access such data.  Should the data be subject to 
HIPAA, the grantee agrees to enter into FTF’s HIPAA Business Associate Agreement. 
 
To inform clients of FTF’s reporting requirements, all grantees must include in their client enrollment 
forms the statement:  “To comply with reporting requirements of the funding source, I grant permission 
to [insert grantee organizational name] to release background, service, and impact related information 
to the Arizona Early Childhood Development and Health Board, also known as First Things First.”  The 
grantee warrants to FTF that prior to entering into the grant agreement for FTF funding, it has 
appropriately enquired and satisfied itself that it has the ability and authority comply with the 
requirements of this section. 
 
Grantees Serving Clients on Tribal Lands 
First Things First recognizes Arizona tribes as sovereign nations that have the right to regulate research 
and data collection on their tribal lands. To this end, First Things First is committed to obtaining all 
appropriate tribal approvals for data collection, analysis and reporting.  Accordingly, grantees shall only 
collect, use and share data from tribal land in accordance with a data collection agreement between a 
tribe and First Things First or the grantee.   
 
Compliance with Data Security Guidelines

<!-- page 463 -->
GRA-RC001-18-0884-01        Page 27 of 27 
The grantee acknowledges that failure to comply with any requirement of these Data Security 
Guidelines shall be a material breach of the grant agreement.  For a copy of First Things First’s own Data 
Security Policy & Procedures and/or Tribal Data Policy, email a request to grants@azftf.gov. 
Revised November 2015

CONTRACT 
 
THIS CONTRACT  is made and entered into as of September 13, 2017  (the 
“Effective Date”) by and betwee n Navajo County (“County”) and Sunland Asphalt & 
Construction, Inc. (“Contractor”). 
 
1. SCOPE OF WORK : Contractor shall furnish any and all materials, equipment and 
labor required for performing all work for HOT ASPHALTIC CONCRETE 
PAVEMENT OVERLAY, CONTRACT, # B17-07-020, in accordance with the 
Contract Documents, in a workmanlike manner to the satisfaction  of County 
through its Engineers. 
 
2. CONTRACT DOCUMENTS : The Contract Documents comprise this Contract and 
the attached  Call for Bids,  Specifications,  General Provisions,  Special 
Provisions, Proposal dated August 30, 2017  Bidding Schedule,  Bid Bond, 
 Performance Bond,  No Collusion Affidavit,  Subcontracting Certification,  
Plans, and addenda thereto, if any.  If there is any conflict in the documents, this 
Contract shall control over all others; the Specifications shall control over the 
General Provisions and Special Provisions; and the Special Provisions shall 
control over the General Provisions. 
 
3. NO LIENS / TIME FOR COMPLETION : Contractor shall furnish all mate rials, 
equipment and labor in accordance with the Contract Documents free and clear of 
all claims, liens and charges whatsoever, within the time, or times, stated in the 
Proposal. 
 
4. CANCELLATION: This Contract is subject to cancellation pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-
511 (concerning conflicts of interest). 
 
5. PAYMENTS:  Payment shall be made on the basis of the unit price(s) bid for the 
awarded items in the Bidding Schedule for Contract #  B17-07-020.  Contractor 
shall submit invoices to County for all work performed hereunder.  No later than 14 
days after each invoice has been verified and approved by the Engineer, County 
shall pay to Contractor 100% of the approved amount.   Retention will not typically 
be withheld unless portions of the work are unsatisfactory or test report(s) are 
incomplete. 
 
6. CERTIFICATIONS: 
 
A. Pursuant to A.R.S. § 41 -4401, Contractor and its subcontractors warrant 
compliance with all federal immigration laws and regulations relating  to their 
employees and compliance with the E -verify requirements under A.R.S. § 23 -
214(A).  Contractor's or any subcontractor's breach of this warranty shall be 
deemed a material breach of the Contract and may result in termination of the 
Contract by Coun ty.  County may randomly inspect the books and records of 
Contractor and its subcontractors to verify compliance with this warranty.  
Contractor and its subcontractors warrant to keep their books and records open 
for random inspection by County during normal business hours.

Board of Supervisors Regular 6.b.        
Meeting Date: 09/12/2017  
Title: Linden Storage SUP 
Submitted For: Glenn Kephart, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Sandra Phillips, Assistant County Engineer/Flood Control Manager
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Presented By: Bill Bess
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 31-17 
approving/denying a Special Use Permit to allow for the construction of a self-mini storage unit
facility that will accommodate a commercial buffer, individual storage units, and covered storage
spaces for boats and RVs on a parcel which is zoned C-R on the front half and A-General for the
back half, APN 409-20-015P, a portion of Township 10 North, Range 21 East, Section 4, in
the Show Low area. 
Background:
The applicant is requesting a Special Use Permit to allow for the construction of a self-mini
storage unit facility where the subject parcel is located immediately north of Highway 260.  The
Navajo County Comprehensive Plan, characterizes the area as Rural Edge.  (Section 4.4.3 of the
Comprehensive Plan.)  The character of Rural Edge is low to medium density residential and
limited commercial uses.  Commercial uses should be allowed and is encouraged at Highway
intersections.  Industrial and Storage uses should be screened from view from rights-of-way.  The
proposed project fits within the recommendations of the Navajo County Comprehensive Plan. 
The proposed use would be permitted in the C-R/A-General zoning district by approval of a
Special Use Permit.  The storage facility will create a slight increase in traffic as the existing lot
has only a mobile home on it.  Planning & Zoning Commission recommended approval to the
Board by a vote of 5 yeas and 1 nay.
 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval of this Special Use Permit under the following stipulations: 
This Special Use Permit shall permit the development of a mini-storage and RV/Boat
storage facility with an office on the subject property. 
1.
The permitted special use shall be allowed to occur only in the location shown on the
approved site plan.
2.
Drainage and grading plans for all new site facilities shall be completed and approved by
Navajo County Public Works prior to the issuance of a building permit for any of the new
structures.
3.
Signs are to conform to the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance.4.

<!-- page 483 -->
Signage must be posted indicating that the storage of dangerous or hazardous materials is
prohibited.
5.
Property must be fenced and screened to a height of 6’ to reduce visual impacts to
surrounding properties.
6.
Security gating and/or on-site management must be provided.7.
Security lighting shall be directed downward, shielded, preventing light trespass.8.
Traffic routing signs should be clearly visible.9.
Must meet approval of the Fire District with jurisdiction, regarding widths of roads,
circulation and other issues before building permits will be issued.
10.
The Special Use Permit shall run with the land.11.
No building or structure may be occupied prior to complete compliance with all appropriate
Building Department requirements.
12.
Construction of this facility must commence within a twelve (12) month period of time
following Board of Supervisors approval.  Failure to do so will result in a review by the
Board for possible revocation of the Special Use Permit.
13.
No RV’s or Trailers shall be placed on site until all site improvements are complete.14.
All required Federal, State, ADOT and Navajo County permits must be obtained and
adhered to prior to construction of facility.
15.

To Whom it May Concern: 
With the ever growing population and development in the western part of Show Low and 
neighboring Linden, I feel there is a need for an additional self storage facility with covered RIV 
parking in this area and it would be a positive and beneficial improvement to the undeveloped 
frontage property I own at 6950 State Route 260, located approximately at mile post 335.1. 
In addition to the self storage facility to be locate.cl to the rear of this location, there would be an 
excellent opportunity for future development of the resulting 16,000+ sq. foot commercial pad 
with a 330 foot highway frontage exposure located at the front of said property. 
Colliers International is a valuation and advisory service company and according to their current 
feasibility study, this project would be a needed and successfu l venture. 
If this permit is granted, construction bids will be solicited with development to start as 
contractor's time schedu les and weather permit. 
Access is easily made from Chamber lain Parkway off of Highway 260. Since this property has 
never been developed with it's own access, a permit will be sought for it's construction to the 
easternmost part of this location as well. 
Sincerely ,

NAVAJO N 
 
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT 
PLANNING & ZONING 
 
 
STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: September 12, 2017 
Case No: 17-21 
Action : Special Use Permit 
Applicant/Owner:  Curtis K. Fernau  
Project Name:  Linden Self Storage  
 
PARCEL INFORMATION: 
 
Address: 6950 State Route 260   
  
APN: 409-20-015P 
 
Legal Desc:  Township 10 North, Range 21 East, Section 4, in the Show Low area. 
 
District: IV 
 
Area: Show Low, AZ 
 
Parcel Size: 4.57 Acres 
 
 
 
STATED REASON FOR REQUEST:  The applicant is requesting a Special Use Permit to allow 
for the construction of a self -mini storage unit facility that will accommodate a commercial buffer, 
individual storage units, and covered storage spaces for boats and RVs. The subject parcel is 
located immediately off of Highway 260. 
 
GENERAL CHARACTER OF NEIGHBORHOOD: The subject parcel is zoned C-R which is 
Commercial-Residential and A-General.  Property to the west and east is also zoned C-R for the 
southern 300 feet, adjacent to Hwy 260.  The properties to the north are zoned A-General.

<!-- page 492 -->
ZONING DISTRICTS:  
 
ARTICLE 15 – (C-R) COMMERCIAL-RESIDENTIAL ZONING DISTRICT    
Section 1501 - Purpose.  
- The principal purpose of this zoning district is to provide for shops and services in 
convenient locations to meet the daily needs of families in the immediate residential 
neighborhoods and wholesaling or distribution activities in locations where there is 
adequate access to major streets or highways. Principal uses permitted in this zoning 
district include retail and wholesale commerce, commercial entertainment, and some uses 
of a light manufacturing nature. 
 
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  The Navajo County Comprehensive Plan, characterizes the area 
as Rural Edge.  (Section 4.4.3 of the Comprehensive Plan.)  The character of Rural Edge is low 
to medium density residential and limited commercial uses.  Commercial uses should be 
allowed and is encouraged at Highway intersections.  Industrial and Storage uses should be 
screened from view from rights-of-way.  The proposed project fits within the recommendations 
of the Navajo County Comprehensive Plan. 
 
CHANGING CONDITIONS:  The proposed use would be permitted in the C-R zoning district by 
approval of a Special Use Permit.  The storage facility will create a slight increase in traffic as the 
existing lot has only a mobile home on it. 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
1. The legal notice for this item has been printed in the local  newspaper, in compliance 
with Arizona Revised Statutes and Article 29 of the Zoning Ordinance. 
 
2. The applicant has submitted the supporting documents required by Section 2902 (1), 
and said supporting documents are complete and adequate for the  Commission’s 
purposes. 
3. The subject property is currently zoned C-R (Commercial-Residential Zoning).  
4. As of today’s date, two (2) comments of concern have been expressed by the public, 
those emails are attached to the packet.  
5. Chamberlain Way is a private ingress/egress easement that would require permission 
from adjacent property owners to utilize. 
 
PUBLIC WORKS AND OTHER COUNTY DEPARTMENT’S COMMENTS:  
 
COUNTY ATTORNEY: 
No objection, as long as the applicant complies with all rules and regulations of Navajo County 
and the State of Arizona. 
Initial:  B.S.C. Date:  8/28/17 
 
ENGINEERING & TRAFFIC: 
Engineering staff has reviewed the proposed Special Use Permit and has no Engineering 
objections given that the conditions listed below are met.  Coordination with ADOT will be 
required.  The ingress/egress easement over Chamberlain Way appears to be private.  Public 
access is needed for this development to proceed. 
Initial: W.R.B. Date:  8/28/17 
 
DRAINAGE & FLOOD CONTROL: 
The Flood Control staff has reviewed the proposed Special Use Permit and has concerns that 
the proposed development of the site can meet current ordinances, but remain confident that 
this can be achieved, given that the conditions listed below are met.  A drainage report will be

<!-- page 493 -->
required prior to permit being released. 
Initial: B.P. Date:   
 
PLANNING & ZONING: 
The Planning & Zoning staff has reviewed the proposed Special Use Permit and has no 
objections, given that the conditions listed below are met. 
Initial: S.P. Date:   
 
 
PLANNING AND ZONING RECOMMENDATION:  Should the Board of Supervisors approve 
this Special Use Permit, staff and the Planning and Zoning Commission recommend (5-1 vote) 
the following conditions be applied (with corrections for conflict in text shown in green below): 
 
1. This Special Use Permit shall permit the development of a mini-storage and RV/Boat storage facility 
with an office on the subject property. 
 
2. The permitted special use shall be allowed to occur only in the location shown on the approved site 
plan. 
 
3. Drainage and grading plans for all new site facilities shall be completed and approved by Navajo 
County Public Works prior to the issuance of a building permit for any of the new structures. 
 
4. Signs are to conform to the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance. 
 
5. Signage must be posted indicating that the storage of dangerous or hazardous materials is 
prohibited. 
 
6. Property must be fenced and screened to a height of 6’ to reduce visual impacts to surrounding 
properties. 
 
7. Security gating and/or on-site management must be provided. 
 
8. Security lighting shall be directed downward, shielded, preventing light trespass. 
 
9. Traffic routing signs should be clearly visible.  
 
10. Must meet approval of the Fire District with jurisdiction, regarding widths of roads , circulation and 
other issues before building permits will be issued. 
 
11. The Special Use Permit shall run with the land, once the development meets all requirements. 
 
12. No building or structure may be occupied prior to complete compliance with all appropriate Building 
Department requirements. 
 
13. Construction of this facility must commence within a twelve (12) month period of time fo llowing 
Board of Supervisors approval.  Failure to do so will result in a review by the Board for possible  
revocation of the Special Use Permit. 
 
14. No RV’s or Trailers shall be placed on site until all site improvements are complete. 
 
15. All required Federal, S tate, ADOT and Navajo County permits must be obtained and adhered to 
prior to construction of facility.

Sir,  
 
For whatever reason the below notice and pdfs were sent to my neighbor on my north side but not to me.  The project 
does not abut my neighbor's property but it does mine! 
 
I need to know that block wall fencing will be put up on the eastern side of the project.  At this time I get a mass of 
garbage and tumbleweeds from that property because the existing business does not police its garbage or care for the 
property in a way that keeps tumbleweeds from forming and inundating my property.  RV owners will bring a ton of 
garbage with them to the storage facility.  When the owner fails to police the garbage it will be all over my property as 
the wind is usually straight from that property to mine at 1028 Pine Ridge Dr.  I am against this proposal if there is not 
going to be a tall block fence at the expense of the facility owner to ensure I'm not looking at crap and to ensure my 
privacy from gawkers parking and picking up their vehicles.  Further if they are allowed to come and go at any time of 
day night then it is completely wrong for a residential area.  I certainly don't want their headlights in my windows at 
night! I bought in a rural area to avoid things such as this. The site plan has the RV parking facing straight into my 
windows!!! 
 
Please let me know what fencing is planned and if possible make them put the RV parking elsewhere and the storage 
units on the east side of their property to reduce loss of privacy for me and my neighbors.  Every person owing property 
on the east side of this proposed project should receive the site plan and notice.  Why I did not is real cause for cencern. 
 
 
Barbara J Karr

Dear Ms. Phillips; 
Thank you for taking my call regarding the hearing to be held on July 20, 2017.   I have several concerns 
regarding the proposed use for the above project.   Please let me detail six of them below.   I have 
circled in dark blue the location of the proposed project APN 409-20-015P which abuts Highway 260.   I 
have also outlined the five parcels that I hold in trust at the lower portion of Chamberlain Way.   
APN 409-20-030               APN 409-20-031         APN 409-20-032 
APN 409-20-012A            APN 409-20-033  
 
                                   
1) As we discussed on the phone, Chamberlain Way does NOT adjoin into Pearce Drive but is a no 
outlet road that continues to parallel straight along parcel 12A and dog legs left to access 
parcels 32, 31, 30 and 33.

<!-- page 498 -->
2 | P a g e  
 
 
2) Chamberlain Way is deeded as a private (non-county maintained) road. I have included both the 
original Permit to Access from Highway and the current road agreement(s) I received in escrow

<!-- page 499 -->
3 | P a g e  
 
when the property was purchased in Sept 2014.  These documents do not show parcel 409-20-
015P as a participant in the easement and we would ask they NOT be allowed to access their 
property from this private road. 
 
3)  
4)

<!-- page 500 -->
4 | P a g e

<!-- page 501 -->
5 | P a g e

<!-- page 502 -->
6 | P a g e  
 
3) Should APN 409-20-015P be found to be a participant in the Chamberlain Way road easement then 
we ask that the conditional use permit be amended to include required annual road maintenance for 
Chamberlain Way ingress and egress from Highway 260 onto parcel 409-20-015P to include grading and 
re-rocking as increased usage would be expected from this commercial project.  We would also request 
seasonal clearing of all ditches for drainage. 
4)  Please require an extensive grading and water control plan for this project.    Drainage is a very 
serious concern as the Fernau parcel is at an altitude of 6,205 (Yellow Highlighted parcel) and the terrain 
drops to an altitude of 6,149 on my property a decline of 56 feet in a quarter of a mile.  There are 
currently two box drains that parallel the Chamberlain Way access.  Highlghted Blue on the East leading 
into a natural runoff channel starting on parcel 409-20-15N that circles my house to the east and 
Highlighted Red on the West with a culvert under Chamberlain Way on lot 409-20-34.   Since drainage 
circles my home I am very very concerned with runoff.   Previously Fernau did some grading and caused 
a serious mud and water runoff to the east drain of Chamberlain Way impacting APN 40-20-015L.   He 
currently has dug a pond with berms that prevent his runoff from travelling down Chamberlain Way.   
Sheet flow run-off from this parcel has caused serious problems in the past.  Should he continue to 
maintain a pond we ask that he be required to perform insect control during the mosquito breeding 
season.   
 
5) The modular home in the picture above (on the right APN 409-20-033) with the circular drive around 
is currently being leased by Timber Mesa Fire and Medical and this lease is expected to continue 
through March 2018.  This building is the temporary firehouse for station #17 while the original 
firehouse located on Highway 260 is being remodeled.    It is critical that we have open road access for 
the firetruck at Chamberlain Way and Highway 260 during and after construction of the Fernau project.   
Please make unimpared road access a condition of all future construction.

<!-- page 503 -->
7 | P a g e  
 
 
6) Last and critically important would be the impact of exterior lighting and noise that this project 
would impose upon the residents of Chamberlain Way.   
a. We would ask that the lighting plan be required to direct light away from our homes and 
windows and where possible the use of motion detectors be used to limit the duration the 
lights remain on.  
b. A reasonable height limit be placed on all lights so that our night sky  panarama is not 
disrupted. 
c. Noise concerns can best be minimized with limited operating hours.  We would ask that this 
facility not be available after 10PM or before 6AM daily.   
 
I do plan on attending the public hearing on July 20, 2017.  If I can provide any further information prior, 
please let me know. 
 
 
Sincerely, 
 
Kristina Petersen 
Cell    951-551-6078
