From: Judy Lauderbaach <lauderbachconstruction@gmail.com>
Sent: Wednesday, July 19, 2017 7:42 PM
To: Sandra Phillips
Subject: Letter to Planning and Zoning 07122017 APN 409-20-015P
To Ms. Sandra Phillips, 
 
 
My name is Kent Lauderbach and I live at 1030 Chamberlain way in Linden. I am emailing in regards to the 
Fernau project  APN 409-20-015P and some of the serious concerns I have regarding his request of a special 
use permit on his Linden property. This is pertinent to me because this property is adjacent  to two of the 
properties that  Iown, one of which being my residence.   
 
The first issue that I would like to address is whether or not he would be able to make use of all of his property 
coming all the way back to the North end  with the special use permit without any type of buffer between this 
project and the residences surrounding it. 
 
My second concern is that there would be a serious drainage problem. As the road comes back towards my 
property chamberlain way drops off much lower than the height of his property and the North West part of his 
property slopes off towards chamberlain, not towards the highway. Approximately 2 summers ago Mr. Fernau 
stripped that entire property of all vegetation, trees, bushes, and shrubs which lead to flooding of our properties 
in the monsoon season. His remedy to that issue was to build and extensive catch pond at the Northwest part of 
his property that is adjust to our properties. He is continuing to do dirt work on his property  to this day without 
any type of permit or engineering on it. 
 
I would respectfully request that possibly yourself or any of the board members could drive down Chamberlain 
Way and have a visual on the lay of his land in regards to some of our concerns. 
  
For the majority of my life I have been in the construction business I have an idea of what it would take to 
execute this project. In order to make it drain towards the highway he would need retaining walls  which would 
then create a massive wall that would be adjacent  to our property, which in effect be extremely detrimental to 
the value of our property. Potentially the restriction of size could be something that could be considered to 
prevent this from happening. Keeping it more towards the highway where commercial would be less 
detrimental to the surrounding properties. 
 
Another major concern is whether or not he has the resources to do this type of project, as well as the ability to 
do this in a timely manner.  
 
I do plan on being at the hearing to personally address my concerns. I would like to take this opportunity to 
thank you for your time and consideration of our concerns regarding this matter.  
 
Regards and thanks 
Kent Lauderbach 
 
I can be contacted at any time at 928-521-0653 or by email at lauderbachconstruction@gmail.com

From: curtis fernau <fernauc@yahoo.com>

Subject: Re: Special Use Permit Application
Hi Sandra, thanks for the e-mail. 
 
I was also at the meeting and was disappointed that I didn't get a chance to speak. I will again go to 
ADOT now and check on the HWY 260 access and take along this mail from you, maybe it will get 
this accomplished.  I asked about it before and they told me that since there was no activity on the 
property, they don't put an approach and entry to vacant land.  
 With a permit to build a facility, I presume I could get a permit to build an entrance. 
 
Chamberlain is an easement for several property owners and homes in the area, including, I thought, 
to mine also.  There was an entry from Chamberlain to my property on the north end, but I removed 
the culvert a couple years ago when I put in the water detention for rainwater run-off.   
There are a couple water meters on my side of Chamberlain and my understanding is that anywhere 
along Chamberlain is access for any of the property owners that border it. The plans also show a gate 
at the northwest corner.  This is an emergency exit location, not one that would be open for 
customer's normal use and I would prefer it not to be there, but I think it is required. 
 
In a permit to build, there will be an engineered plan to retain/contain excess water run-off from my 
property, right now the property has never been developed, 
There are many self storage facilities with residential housing bordering them and a lot closer than my 
plan and I would strive make sure my neighbor's concerns were addressed to their satisfaction, 
including normal hours of operation and lighting that did not disturb them.  Actually, disturbance from 
self storage facilities is a lot less than residential housing.  Self storage customers have an average o
f 
six (6) visits a year and that procedure usually is not one that causes a disturbance to anyone. 
 
I appreciate your e-mail and will attend the next meeting and try to calm my neighbors if they are 
there also.  I passed them as I was leaving and wish I had more time to stop, meet and discuss their 
concerns that evening, but I had other commitments and was late as it was.  I didn't plan on or had 
any idea the meeting would take so much time or I would have enjoyed talking to them.  I will plan 
accordingly next month. 
 
This property has never been developed and I want to see it improved and elevate the value of all the 
property owner's investments.  Just to the west, the container business has operated there for a few 
years and I want to have a much nicer appeal than they now have.  They approached me several 
years ago to lease my property when they were in the Show Low Commercial area behind Show Low 
Ford, and I refused as I didn't want to see containers from the highway.  A lot of good that did, they 
just opened up a hundred yards down the road and extend probably just as far north as my plan 
shows. 
 
Believe me, I want to build a nice facility here, one that enhances the area and not disturb my 
neighbors, after all, some may be future customers.

Your application was continued last night as there was confusion on the zoning.  We have Resolution 04-04.  If you have 
the resolution that grants the entire lot the zone change, that would be helpful. 
  
We also had concern about the access from Hwy 260.  Have you spoken to ADOT? Can you provide a letter that they will 
be allowing a driveway into the property? 
  
Our research shows that Chamberlain is a private easement.  Do you have permission to use this easement?  Can you 
please update your site plan reflecting circulation, and access as approved by ADOT?

taken into consideration in your site plan.  What is your plan for the slopes?  They are also concerned about lighting.  If 
you could let us know what your intent is on lighting and hours of access.

th.  I think it would be a good idea if you were there to speak to the Commission on 
what you have planned.

Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 
RESOLUTION ____-17 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
APPROVING A SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR CURTIS FERNAU 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit was duly filed on June 26, 2017  by Curtis 
K. Fernau  requesting a Special Use Permit on the current C-R/A-General zoned parcel to allow 
for the construction of a mini storage and commercial pad on north side of Highway 260; and 
 
WHEREAS the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County: 
APN: 409-20-015P a portion of T10N, R21E, Section 4, in the Linden area; and 
 
WHEREAS the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on August 17, 2017; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony and other 
evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of staff, found that the Special 
Use Permit , is consistent with the public health, safety and gener al welfare and should be 
approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Supervisors  hereby approves the 
Special use permit, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. This Special Use  Permit shall permit the development of a mini -storage and RV/Boat 
storage facility with an office on the subject property. 
 
2. The permitted special use shall be allowed to occur only in the location shown on the 
approved site plan. 
 
3. Drainage and grading plans for all new site facilities shall be completed and approved by 
Navajo County Public Works prior to the issuance of a building permit for any of the new 
structures. 
 
4. Signs are to conform to the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance. 
 
5. Signage must be posted indi cating that the storage of dangerous or hazardous materials is 
prohibited. 
 
6. Property must be screened with a wall, a maximum combined height of 6’  (measured 
from top of footing to top of wall)  to reduce visual impacts to surrounding properties.

<!-- page 516 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
Wall shall be earth tones.  If multiple walls are needed to screen the facility, a 6’ 
horizontal distance between walls, with this area landscaped. 
 
7. Security gating and/or on-site management must be provided. 
 
8. Security lighting shall be directed downward, shielded, preventing light trespass. 
 
9. Traffic routing signs should be clearly visible.  
 
10. Must meet approval of the Fire District with jurisdiction, regarding widths of roads, 
circulation and other issues before building permits will be issued. 
 
11. The Special Use  Permit shall run with the land, once the development meets all 
requirements. 
 
12. No building or structure may be occupied prior to complete compliance with all 
appropriate Building Department requirements. 
 
13. Construction of this facility must commence within a twelve (12) month period of time 
following Board of Supervisors approval.  Failure to do so will result in revocation of the 
Special Use Permit. 
 
14. No RV’s or Trailers shall be placed on site until all site improvements are complete. 
 
15. All required Federal,  State, ADOT and Navajo County permits must be obtained and 
adhered to prior to construction of facility. 
 
 
 
PASSED AND ADOPTED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors by a vote of _____ yeas 
and ______ nays on this 12th  day of September 2017.  
 
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
 
 By ________________________________________ 
                                                                           Jesse Thompson, Chairman of the Board 
ATTEST: 
 
________________________________ 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board

APPROVING A SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR CURTIS FERNAU 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit was duly filed on June 26, 2017   by Curtis 
K Fernau  requesting a Special Use Pe rmit on the current C-R/A-General zoned parcel to allow 
for the construction of a mini storage and commercial pad north of Highway 260; and

<!-- page 518 -->
Board of Supervisors Regular 6.c.        
Meeting Date: 09/12/2017  
Title: Perkins Valley & Mesa Hills Estates Final Plat Extension 
Submitted By: Nick Coussoulis, Planner 1
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Presented By: Bill Bess
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 32-17
approving/denying Final Plat Extensions on two (2) residential projects that were initiated in
2006, on a total of 46.14 acres currently zoned Special Development and R1-43, north of
Interstate 40 and west of Holbrook, on Township 18 North, Range 20 East, Section 33. 
Background:
The applicant (Mark Herrmann), is requesting an additional final plat extension due to delays on
the two (2) projects located West of Holbrook and North of I-40; Perkins Valley and Mesa Hills
Estates. The surrounding area is a mix of large rural residential, vacant land and commercial
residential parcels. The parcels are just outside the current Holbrook municipal limits. The subject
parcels are currently vacant. The Master Development Plan on Perkins Valley was originally
approved in 2006, Mesa Hills Estates was originally approved in 2008.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval of the Final Plat Extension under the following conditions:
1. Approval is conditional upon all subdivision improvements being substantially completed
within 24 months after the date of the P&Z Commission time extension approval. As this is the
third (3rd) extension, if ground is not broken, grading of site and construction of improvements
begun, it is recommended that the Final Plat expire. If the applicant desires to appeal the
expiration, they can petition to go before the Board of Supervisors, by submitting request to the
Clerk of the Board. 
2. Upon approval of the Final Plat, the subdivision roads will not be accepted into the Navajo
County road maintenance system until they are brought up to meet the Navajo County road
maintenance standard by approval of the Superintendent of Streets, at which time the roads will
automatically be accepted for maintenance by Navajo County with no further action required. 
3. The Final Plat document and CC&R's will not be recorded until all subdivision improvements
have been completed to the satisfaction of the Navajo County Engineer and receipt of a financial
assurances agreement for work within the Navajo County Right-of-Way is approved by the
Navajo County Attorney's office and signed by the Chairman of the Board of Supervisors.

NAVAJO N 
 
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT 
PLANNING & ZONING 
 
 
STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: September 12, 2017 
Case No: 17-17 
Action : Final Plat Extension on Two Projects: Perkins 
Valley Estates & Mesa Hills Estates 
Applicant/Owner:  Pacific Holt Corporation  
PARCEL INFORMATION: 
Address: 4150 Boyse Road   
APN:             109-33-039, 040E, 040F,  109-01-005C 
Legal Desc:  Township 18 North, Range 20 East, Section 33 in the Holbrook area. 
District: II & III 
Area: Holbrook, AZ 
Parcel Size: 46.14 Acres 
 
STATED REASON FOR REQUEST:  The applicant requesting an additional final plat extension 
due to delays on the two (2) projects located west of Holbrook and north of I -40; Perkins Valley 
Estates and Mesa Hills Estates. 
 
GENERAL CHARACTER OF NEIGHBORHOOD: The surrounding area is a mix of large rural 
residential, vacant land and commercial residential parcels.  The parcels are just outside the 
current Holbrook municipal limits.  The subject parcels are currently vacant.

<!-- page 529 -->
The yellow shading is the Holbrook municipal limits. 
The zoning on the Perkins Valley Estates parcel is currently Special Development as the Master 
Site Plan were approved by the Board of Supervisors in 2006.  The zoning on the Mesa Hills 
Estates parcels is R1-43.

<!-- page 530 -->
ZONING DISTRICTS:  
 
ARTICLE 17 – (SD) SPECIAL DEVELOPMENT ZONE   
Section 1701 - Purpose. 
- The purpose of the Special Development zone is to provide for the development of subdivisions and 
other land use projects containing various dwelling types, necessary commercial centers, off street 
parking areas, open space areas for recreation, and to preserve a rural atmosphere.  
- Deviations from design standards for the specific zone or zones may be permitted provided open 
spaces are developed and maintained for the use of the residents and provided further that the 
parcel size, soil conditions, terrain, ground cover and other significant natural features favor this type 
of development. 
 
ARTICLE 9 - (R1-43) SINGLE-FAMILY RESIDENTIAL ZONING DISTRICT  
Section 901 - Purpose.  
- The principal purpose of this zoning district is to conserve and protect single -family residential 
development where minimum lots of not less than forty-three thousand (43,000) square feet in area 
are suitable and appropriate taking into consideration existing conditions, including present use of 
land, present lot sizes, future land use n eeds, and the availability of public utilities. Principal uses 
permitted in this zoning district include single -family dwellings, churches, schools, parks, 
playgrounds, and other community facilities. 
 
 
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  The Navajo County Comprehensive Plan, characterizes the area 
as Rural Ranch.  (Section 4.4.1 of the Comprehensive Plan.)

<!-- page 531 -->
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
1. The legal notice for this item has been printed in the local  newspaper, in compliance 
with Arizona Revised Statutes and Article 29 of the Zoning Ordinance. 
 
2. The applicant has submitted the supporting documents required by Section 2902 (1), 
and said supporting documents are complete and adequate for the  Board’s 
purposes. 
3. The subject property is zoned  SD (Special Development) and R1 -43 (Single-Family 
Residential to 43,000 Square Feet).  
4. The applicant requested an extension of time within the allotted time. 
5. The Final Plats, on both of these subdivisions, were approved in 2013. 
 
PUBLIC WORKS AND OTHER COUNTY DEPARTMENT’S COMMENTS:  
 
COUNTY ATTORNEY: 
No objection, as long as the applicant complies with all rules and regulations of Navajo County 
and the State of Arizona. 
 
Initial:  B.S.C. Date:   
 
ENGINEERING & TRAFFIC: 
Engineering staff has reviewed the proposed Final Plat Extension and has no Engineering 
objections given that the conditions listed below are met. 
 
Initial: W.R.B. Date:   
 
 
DRAINAGE & FLOOD CONTROL: 
The Flood Control staff has reviewed the proposed Final Plat Extension and has no objections, 
given that the conditions listed below are met. 
 
Initial: B.P. Date:  6/14/17 
 
PLANNING & ZONING: 
The Planning & Zoning staff has reviewed the proposed Final Plat Extension and has no 
objections, given that the conditions listed below are met. 
 
Initial: S.P. Date:   
 
 
PLANNING AND ZONING RECOMMENDATION:  Should the Board approve the Final Plat 
Extension, staff recommends the conditions below be applied:   
 
1.  Approval is conditioned upon all subdivision improvements being substantially completed 
within 24 months after the date of the P&Z Commission time extension approval.  As this is the 
third (3rd) extension, if ground is not broken, grading of site and construction of improvements 
begun, it is recommended that the Final Plat expire.  If the applicant desires to appeal the 
expiration, they can petition to go before the Board of Supervisors, by submitting request to the 
Clerk of the Board.

<!-- page 532 -->
2.  Upon approval of the Final Plat, the subdivision roads will not be accepted into the Navajo 
County road maintenance system until they are brought up to meet the Navajo County road 
maintenance standard by approval of the Superintendent of Streets, at which time the roads 
will automatically be accepted for maintenance by Navajo County with no further action 
required. 
 
3.  The Final Plat document and CC&R' s will not be recorded until all subdivision 
improvements have been completed to the satisfaction of the Navajo County Engineer and  
receipt of a financial assurances agreement for work within the Navajo County Right­ of-Way 
is approved by the Navajo County Attorney's office and signed by the Chairman of  the Board 
of Supervisors.

<!-- page 533 -->
RESOLUTION ____-17 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
APPROVING A FINAL PLAT EXTENSION FOR PACIFIC HOLT  
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Final Plat Extension was duly filed on May 3, 2017  by Pacific 
Holt (Mark Herrmann)  requesting to extend the final plat for Perkins Valley and Mesa Hills 
estates on the current SD and R1-43 zoned parcels; and 
 
WHEREAS the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County: 
APN: 109-33-039, 040E, 040F, 109 -01-005C a portion of T 18N, R 20E, Section 33 , in the 
Holbrook area; and 
 
WHEREAS the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on August 17, 2017; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony and other 
evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of staff, found that the Final 
Plat Extension, is consistent with the public health, safety and gener al welfare and should be 
approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Supervisors hereby approves the Final 
Plat Extension, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1.  Approval is conditioned upon all subdivision improvements being substantially completed 
within 24 months after the date of the P&Z Commission time extension approval.  As this is 
the third (3rd) extension, if ground is not broken, grading of site and construction of 
improvements begun, it is recommended that the Final Plat expire.  If the applicant desires to 
appeal the expiration, they can petition to go before the Board of Supervisors, by submitting 
request to the Clerk of the Board.  
 
2.  Upon approval of the Final Plat, the subdivision roads will not be accepted into the Navajo 
County road maintenance system until they are brought up to meet the Navajo County road 
maintenance standard by approval of the Superintendent of Streets, at which time the roads 
will automatically be accepted for maintenance by Navajo County with no further action 
required. 
 
3.  The Final Plat document and CC&R's will not be recorded until all subdivision 
improvements have been completed to the satisfaction of the Navajo County Engineer 
and receipt of a financial assurances agreement for work within the Navajo County

<!-- page 534 -->
Right­of-Way is approved by the Navajo County Attorney's office and signed by the 
Chairman of the Board of Supervisors. 
 
 
PASSED AND ADOPTED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors by a vote of _____ yeas 
and ______ nays on this 12th day of September 2017.  
 
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS

<!-- page 535 -->
RESOLUTION ____-17 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS DENYING 
A FINAL PLAT EXTENSION FOR PACIFIC HOLT 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Final Plat Extension was duly filed on May 3, 2017   by Pacific 
Holt (Mark Herrmann)  requesting to e xtend the final plat for Perkins Valley and Mesa Hills 
estates on the current SD and R1-43 zoned parcels; and 
 
WHEREAS the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County: 
APN: 109-33-039, 040E, 040F, 109 -01-005C, a portion of T18 N, R 20E, Section 33, in the 
Holbrook area; and 
 
WHEREAS the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on August 17, 2017; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the  testimony and other 
evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of staff, found that the Final 
Plat Extension, is not consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare and should be 
denied; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Supervisors hereby deny the Final Plat 
Extension. 
 
DENIED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors by a vote of _____ yeas and ______ nays 
on this 12th day of September 2017.  
 
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
